/home/mrnob0dy666/imsgct/p4radice/corpus/text.md:104673:**Door 4 — small molecule (`lift_smiles`).** A molecule given as a SMILES string has no residue order to walk, so its word is the marks its functional groups carry, in the canonical order of the twelve. Chirality — invisible to an achiral group census — is reported separately, as a *CIP word on ⊥*: one bit per stereocenter (1 = R, 0 = S). *Worked examples:* glucose ≺⋈⊤◻ with CIP 11100 (five centers: D-glucose); cellobiose ≺⋈⊤◻ with CIP 1001001100; ATP ⊢≺⋈⊤∈⊙⊥⊞; glycerol ≺⋈⊤. /home/mrnob0dy666/imsgct/p4radice/corpus/text.md:104778:**7.6 The molecular clock of aging: asparagine deamidation as frame loss.** Deamidation of asparagine — spontaneous conversion of the sidechain amide to a carboxyl (Asn → Asp/isoAsp) — is a documented molecular clock in protein aging: it accumulates in eye-lens crystallins, in amyloid plaques, and in long-lived proteins generally, and it is a single-atom change that somehow does disproportionate damage. The grammar gives that damage a mechanism. In the bijection, Asn is ∈ (FSPLIT, the *opening* of a frame) and Asp is ⊥ (EVALF, the chirality mark): deamidation converts a frame operator into a chirality operator. The aged protein literally loses the ability to close its loops — the frames it opened when it was translated can no longer be paid. Gln → Glu deamidation is the symmetric loss: ∋ (closure) becomes ◻ (fixation), so what should close becomes merely frozen. The residue pair evolution chose as the frame pair is, fatefully, the pair whose amide chemistry cannot be held stable — the clock is built into the code.

/home/mrnob0dy666/imsgct/p4radice/corpus/text.md:126833:- `imasm` — Write a tuple to its word, derive a word back to its tuple, and run the kernel's instruments on it — one QEMU boot. WRITE: imasm(tuple='𐑦𐑸𐑾𐑹𐑐𐑧𐑲𐑠⊙𐑫𐑳𐑭') gives the word that tuple composes to. DERIVE: imasm(word='⊢⊙∈⊤⊥∋◻⊣', derive=true) gives the tuple it imscribes to. Never hand-pick glyphs: both directions are deterministic and the kernel owns them. IMASM is a language you WRITE: a word IS its structural type, so imscribing a structure and running this on it settles what prose can only assert. The twelve marks are ⊢ ⊣ > < ⋈ ⊤ ∈ ∋ ⊙ ⊥ ⊞ ◻ — VINIT open, TANCH close, AFWD advance, AREV the clearing reverse, CLINK compose, IMSCRIB self-reference, FSPLIT/FFUSE the δ/μ frame pair, EVALT/EVALF deposit T/F, ENGAGR the Belnap diagonal, IFIX fix. Retired marks ◇ ● + × = ¬ are not tokens and are refused. OPS: weight (the movement trace — every clear, seed, frame open, deposit and fuse, with the final register and what survived); banked (did a clear fire against a live register with nothing banked — VACUOUS means nothing was ever at risk); cycle (the ROTAT orbit: period, landing register per cut, whether the word is phase-bearing); insert (every one-glyph repair for an exposed word); trans (transitions on the ring, closing edge included). THE COMPOSITION RULE: δ before δ, μ after μ — open the frame BEFORE the counting and close it AFTER the reversal. A count held in the open when an AREV fires is lost; banked in an enclosing frame it survives and the fuse restores it. Batch several ops: the boot dominates, the ops are free.

/home/mrnob0dy666/imsgct/p4radice/corpus/text.md:536753:**Door 4 — small molecule (`lift_smiles`).** A molecule given as a SMILES string has no residue order to walk, so its word is the marks its functional groups carry, in the canonical order of the twelve. Chirality — invisible to an achiral group census — is reported separately, as a *CIP word on ⊥*: one bit per stereocenter (1 = R, 0 = S). *Worked examples:* glucose ≺⋈⊤◻ with CIP 11100 (five centers: D-glucose); cellobiose ≺⋈⊤◻ with CIP 1001001100; ATP ⊢≺⋈⊤∈⊙⊥⊞; glycerol ≺⋈⊤. Two molecules in this study *failed* their verdict: cholesterol (⊣≺⋈⊤∋) and tripalmitin (⊣≻⋈∋) both returned **F**, "a fuse with no fork" — a finding about the census, discussed in Section 8.

/home/mrnob0dy666/imsgct/p4radice/corpus/text.md:536788:- **closed by one appended μ** `⊢⊞◻◻⋈⊤◻⊥⊥∈⊙⊞⋈∋` → **T**, *"Tri-ancestral reconnection over a transformed object — closes."*
/home/mrnob0dy666/imsgct/p4radice/corpus/text.md:536813:On the closed GFP word the kernel's movement trace shows: the opening ⊢ clears an empty register (nothing lost — the chain starts from nothing); the diagonal ⊞ *deposits both T and F at once* — the chain's state is both-true-and-false until the frame closes it; and eight marks are inert (the no-ops and the frame pair itself). The `banked` instrument reports **VACUOUS** — no clear ever fired against a live register — because the fixation ◻ freezes the result before any deposit is at risk. The general rule, in chemical terms: a chain that never banks its register in an enclosing frame loses everything at the first reversal (hydrolysis, stop, cleavage). The frame is the chemical bond; the bank is the fold.

/home/mrnob0dy666/imsgct/p4radice/corpus/text.md:536858:**7.6 The molecular clock of aging: asparagine deamidation as frame loss.** Deamidation of asparagine — spontaneous conversion of the sidechain amide to a carboxyl (Asn → Asp/isoAsp) — is a documented molecular clock in protein aging: it accumulates in eye-lens crystallins, in amyloid plaques, and in long-lived proteins generally, and it is a single-atom change that somehow does disproportionate damage. The grammar gives that damage a mechanism. In the bijection, Asn is ∈ (FSPLIT, the *opening* of a frame) and Asp is ⊥ (EVALF, the chirality mark): deamidation converts a frame operator into a chirality operator. The aged protein literally loses the ability to close its loops — the frames it opened when it was translated can no longer be paid. Gln → Glu deamidation is the symmetric loss: ∋ (closure) becomes ◻ (fixation), so what should close becomes merely frozen. The residue pair evolution chose as the frame pair is, fatefully, the pair whose amide chemistry cannot be held stable — the clock is built into the code.
